rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on employment and social affairs. − madam president, the negotiations on ipa were harder than they should have been, i think. we are all in favour of this instrument that encourages reform and helps accession countries get closer to the eu. ipa ii, i think, is an improvement on the previous one, with a less strict division between the pillars and more possibilities for smaller organisations and municipalities in the region to carry out smaller projects. i hope that balkan countries will grab these opportunities. i hope that, for instance, montenegro will develop ecologically-friendly tourism in the north while at the same time training unemployed people to work in this field. none of that was the problem in the ipa negotiations. one of the main problems was how to refer to kosovo because of the five non-recognisers. to the ordinary citizens that i told about this, the issue seems totally insane – and that is because it is. let me therefore conclude by calling on the five eu member states that do not recognise kosovo for reasons that have nothing whatsoever to do with kosovo. please stop holding back a small new country in its efforts to reform, to be a modern european member state. even serbia is being more conciliatory, more sensible and, i dare say, more mature about this than you are. so take your example from them and stop being ridiculous.
madam president, how often have we heard ministers, the high representative and others speak about how more for more would frame a more values-based eu policy towards neighbouring countries? after years of interest-based approaches at the expense of people’s rights and after all these speeches and two years of negotiation, one would have expected a better european neighbourhood instrument than the one we see today. financing has a flexibility of 10% up or down and this house wanted more of an incentive. additionally, the commission and the council chose control and the status quo on a bureaucratic level over more flexibility in the programmes so that we can actually ensure that money ends up benefiting people in countries from egypt to the ukraine. transparency and accountability need to be in place to prevent repetitions of the report of the court of auditors on egypt, which raised questions on whether the eu funds accomplish our goals. i believe the people in europe’s neighbourhood now more than ever need our support based on european values such as democracy, respect for human rights and based on the rule of law. governments of these countries sadly are not the best interlocutors as they do not have the well-being of their people in mind at all times.
- i'm the reporter. - in this whole discussion, we seem to agree on the principles of the european union's external policy worldwide. there is a coherent approach to each of the components of this external policy. it is only to be hoped that geopolitical strategic interests will be less of an obstacle to poverty reduction than they have been up to now. but all too often, stability in the region is more important than tackling corrupt regimes. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. helmut scholz rightly pointed that out. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. for coherence, the noses in the commission, the member states and parliament should indeed be pointed in the same direction. our commercial and industrial interests must not interfere with the opportunities of poor countries. the lisbon treaty also prohibits this. legislation is one thing, but its implementation depends on political will. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat poverty. and yet europe itself is stagnating economically. our opportunities are now outside europe, we must work for more prosperity, more peace and more rights worldwide. the fight against poverty is a moral duty and a european strategic investment.
on behalf of the alde group. – madam president, albania has been taking important steps towards meeting the eu criteria but, while we acknowledge that, this parliament clearly defines the many areas where work needs to be done. our list is long and includes the need to address tackling corruption and the need to reform public administration, eradicating organised crime, improving media freedom and independence, safeguarding the rights of various minorities, including roma and lgbt, overcoming the political polarisation that runs deeply through society, reforming the judiciary and ensuring its independence, promoting a genuine dialogue between civil society and government, and guaranteeing the well-being of those who depend on the state, whether in prison or mentally ill. the liberal group supports the commission’s position on granting albania candidate status, which should not be mistaken for membership of the eu in any way. in election years especially, we must be clear about managing expectations and ensuring that debates about enlargement are fact-based. the same goes for addressing albanian audiences. this is not a reward for one government or another, but rather a signal to all albanians to encourage them to move forward with the many remaining reforms towards meeting the copenhagen criteria. we have the well-being of the people in mind, which should benefit from continuing to walk down the european path.
madam president, i would like to congratulate albania on the most positive report we have been able to write about the country in years. congratulations to mr berisha, who stepped aside after the elections without a fuss, a first in albania’s democratic history, i think. congratulations to mr rama who managed to finally finish albania’s european homework. however, we should not trust that the very recent past of political bickering and paralysis of political process because of the deep divisions between the main parties is completely in the past now. let us commend albania on what has gone well and let us ask to see an extended track record of constructive political cooperation before we shout halleluiah. as a final remark i would like to say that it is quite unhelpful for some members of the s&d group and some members of the ppe group, though absolutely not all, to mix their own party politics with those in albania. there is clearly enough trouble with party politics in albania itself without us taking sides, so let us hope that the ministers will give albania a chance to move ahead in its reform process, and let us hope that the albanian political parties on all sides will take that chance with both hands.
for the epp group on november 30, shirin ebadi and payam akhavan published an op-ed in the washington post under the clear headline "in iran, human rights cannot be sacrificed for a nuclear deal". i also find this message in paragraph 70 of the excellent kukan report. i earnestly hope that all the european institutions, including our own house, in future contacts with official representatives of the islamic republic of iran, will defend the basic fundamental rights of iranian citizens and openly and concretely identify violations. the conclusion of a comprehensive nuclear agreement means that the international community, the european union, cannot remain silent about the alarming increase in the number of executions in recent weeks, about the continuing persecution of religious and ethnic minorities. i think, for example, of the iranian-american pastor abedini. i hope the special representative will work for his release. ladies and gentlemen, take note of the un report on the human rights situation in iran this autumn. for example, on page 6 i read that since the beginning of this year iran has had a special machine that accelerates the execution of amputations in public. how awful!
i have found the past few years as spokesman for my group on the globalisation fund very instructive. the applications were a gauge to see which sectors were in trouble in the crisis, which countries and regions were hit the hardest. unfortunately, the applications also showed that far too little is being done. the decline of the flemish textile industry, for example, could have been predicted long ago. and then one could have made preventive plans to restructure that industry and retrain the workers in time. on the positive side, the european globalisation adjustment fund has brought a lot of good to people in very, very need in a lot of countries. mobility centres have been set up in countries and regions where there were none. a surprising number of people have returned to work, including people over 50. i think we've achieved a good result in the negotiations. the globalisation fund can never replace good and forward-looking labour market policies, but it gives people who lose their jobs in these times of crisis hope and the prospect of new jobs, and that is invaluable right now!
president. - the globalisation fund is facilitating unemployment and favouring large companies over small ones and the self-employed. entrepreneurship involves both opportunities and risks, and it has both prospects and setbacks. the existence of a richly endowed globalisation fund upsets that balance. economic hardship or, for example, the choice of relocating a company, is then met by society rather than by the companies. the globalisation fund therefore distorts competition and places the costs of running large companies on the taxpayer. the commission has also been very active in the fight against unemployment, and has been very active in the fight against unemployment. the pvv sees more than enough reasons to abolish the globalisation fund today.
president, the european union is thinking to itself: let us talk about something else in the midst of the crisis, namely, more military cooperation. the crisis must not, of course, be allowed to diminish the fun here, and so there are plans for eu combat forces, a permanent operational headquarters, joint financing of operations and even plans for european drones. and of course, preferably all paid for by the eu budget. there is a danger that a lot of taxpayers' money will be thrown at the establishment of all sorts of military institutions and bureaucracies within the eu. the european union is all too eager to play the superpower. but the citizen has no use for these expensive and pointless federal hobbies, because the motive behind these plans is, of course, to create a european federal state with its own army. the dutch armed forces must remain entirely in national hands and where we cooperate, we do so bilaterally and within nato. a european union army, that never! i hope that the forthcoming summit on european military cooperation will end in one great disappointment. that is the only good outcome and in the interests of the netherlands. and i would like to say to mr brok in particular: despite your dislike of populist parties, the big turning point will be the elections next may and there will be a new wind in the european parliament.
the next summit, mr president, will focus on european defence policy, or rather on the european defence industry. but economic cooperation and the further development of economic union are also on the agenda. in this respect, fundamental steps are being taken again, as was done last night by the finance ministers, towards an intergovernmentally organized resolution fund, following the example of the intergovernmentally organized emergency fund. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competition' in the context of the directive. the european summit seems to be blind to democratic control of the european commission. the european commission is gaining more and more power and political control is increasingly being exercised by the european commission. when will we really ensure that the european commission is democratically controlled? in conclusion: the social europe. there are two meagre paragraphs in the conclusions on a social europe. does europe have a social future, or does it not, according to the leaders of the governments?
mr president, i still do not understand why we are talking about housework in this parliament. let it be. i would like to speak about the fact that migrants who want to work in domestic work must have a valid employment contract or some other form of proof as soon as they cross the border. i would like to see this in an even broader perspective, namely that every migrant from any eu member state who wants to settle and work in an average european country must have a valid employment contract before crossing the border. that is unfortunately an illusion for the future!
in writing. − while i believe this is an improvement on the previous programme, i do not support this outcome of the negotiations. development policy is first and foremost a national task for the member states. the eu should focus on coordinating the assistance provided by the member states.
mr president, when i came to this parliament in 2009, i was a member of the committee on budgets. one of the first meetings asked 'do you have any ideas for the european globalisation adjustment fund, because the money is running out?' there was 500 million euros in that pot. it is now 429 million euros, but it is still a lot of money for a fund which is actually a very strange vehicle. the fund is designed to respond to the liberalization of markets in europe. on the one hand, we say: more member states, open borders, free movement of goods, people, services. on the other hand, we say, if there is scaling up, if there is liberalisation, then all of a sudden we have money for people who are unemployed. i think that is a market operation and nothing more, and that is what we should applaud. and if it is used for people who are unemployed as a result, it seems to me that it is better to call the fund a 'victim fund' rather than a globalisation fund.
president. - the member states are bound not only by the treaty of lisbon but also by separate financial agreements. this is an unclear situation in the field of public law, and the rapporteur rightly raises this point. but the rapporteur's counter-proposals only add to the problem. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the european parliament, the council and the commission. the european central bank will thus become a political institution once and for all. mr president, monetary policy is not a political game, but a crucial technical part of our economy. the proposal to allow the european parliament to adopt treaty amendments is also contrary to the sovereign position of the member states. the european parliament is a servant of the treaty, not its master. my party cannot therefore approve this proposal. i am concerned that even the committee on constitutional affairs is now ignoring the treaty. where is the citizen and where is the rule of law in this discussion?
mr president, we are dealing here with a framework for maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal management. it is a directive which we should not be discussing at all. there are no less than 11 subsidiarity concerns raised against this proposal, and they are all justified. the eu is not about spatial planning and this directive is therefore simply contrary to the treaty. the european commission has conveniently ignored all these subsidiarity concerns. it seems that everything is permissible in the face of the further federalisation and the growing power of unelected brussels bureaucrats. the commission is going too far and interfering with national affairs through this invasive directive by packaging policy as necessary eu coordination. the commission then starts to talk about 'coordinated action' and 'sustainable development' and, by justifying its intervention with nonsense about erosion, climate change and natural disasters, it puts the whole thing to sleep. on behalf of the many dutch people who are sick and tired of the eu's increasingly brazen drive for rules, i say to the commission: "stay out of our zoning, pack your bags and go home!"
president. - we are living in a time of financial crisis and we see that every euro, whether public or private, is turned over three times before it is spent. well , in that regard , i have some wonderful news . green infrastructure investments are incredibly lucrative. you might even say: six to get, one to pay. i'll name them for you. especially in and around cities, the effects are enormous. you'll get cleaner air. you give people the opportunity to move. and these two together have a very positive effect on public health. it leads to more social cohesion. it has an excellent effect on biodiversity. you see that real estate, houses, are going up in value. finally, mr president, the business climate is being considerably strengthened. in other words, six take, pay one, when you invest in green infrastructure. so you would expect that investment in green infrastructure is a sine qua non of investment in ordinary grey infrastructure. but unfortunately, nothing could be further from the truth. if you look at the spending through the structural funds, through the social funds, through the other infrastructure works, you see that the green element always comes to the very end, while it has such a terribly positive return. i would therefore also like to call on the commission not only to come here with beautiful stories about natura 2000, about the importance of green infrastructure, but to be much more critical in assessing grey infrastructure and only then to authorize projects if there is also attention to green infrastructure.
president draghi, i would like to thank you for your commitment to shaping and implementing the monetary policy of the euro area in an independent and fair manner. fortunately, the euro storm seems to be coming to an end, but this also threatens to dampen the sense of urgency, and i see this as a great risk. the epp group does not want to be complacent, but to be ambitious in its commitment to growth and stability. this requires structural reforms in member states and the restoration of the free movement of capital. and for the record, let there be no ambiguity about this. this parliament does not want to change the mandate of the ecb! president draghi, you have made your commitment with the establishment of the omt and rightly linked it to strict conditionality, and that also applies to the ltro. a temporary measure, because the structural solution is to restore confidence in our banking sector. money has to roll. this is the best guarantee that banks can resume their role as financiers of small and mediumsized enterprises. president, the ecb is going to take over the banking supervision as the single supervisor. you're on course with the preparation. we were informed of this in detail in frankfurt last monday. but you can't walk on one leg. government leaders have promised that there will also be a single resolution authority, and we as co-legislators are committed to delivering that. you will understand that we are concerned about the course the ecofin ministers seem to be taking. a spin-off of the fund into an intergovernmental body could impair the effectiveness of the resolution authority. what is at stake is that the decision-making on the resolution will be left to the member states rather than to a community authority. and what did not work in the stability and growth pact, which we abolished there, we would then introduce here. mr president, that's not going to work! we will decide our position as parliament next week and we are ready for negotiations with the council.
the ecb took unusual policy measures in 2012 due to the exceptional circumstances. the commission has already taken a number of steps towards a banking union. i object strongly to the following step: a common resolution fund for banks. the account of bad loans on the balance sheets of weak banks is being spread over the europeans, without the size of the account being clear. this problematic process involving the ecb is not mentioned, and probably therefore it is not mentioned in this report on the ecb. in addition to austerity and reform, the correct approach to the euro crisis is ultimately to partially write off loans to governments and write off loans to banks. it will take a lot for the ecb to accompany this with the right monetary policy. however, now that the unrest has subsided, she must return to her core task as soon as possible: the commission has already taken a number of measures. i would have liked to have seen this as the central message of the report.
president. - the treaty provides that the european central bank must be politically independent and must pursue monetary stability exclusively. the treaty leaves no room for other considerations. but the bank has been systematically violating the treaty since the financial crisis, for example by buying government bonds (article 123), for example by lowering interest rates to keep the sovereign debts of the southern member states solvent at the expense of all savers in europe. mr draghi also announced that he would continue to finance weak countries without limitation. and in doing so, the ecb is acting politically and therefore the bank is going beyond its mandate. but this parliament is applauding this violation of the treaties. the commission has also proposed that the council should be given the task of drawing up a report on the situation in the community. a central bank, mr president, cannot be controlled by random political majorities. monetary stability is not a political game. the european union can only rely on the confidence that the european institutions will respect what has been agreed. otherwise, mr president, there is no rule of law.
in writing. - i shall abstain on this resolution. although there are good points in the resolution, the resolution goes too far in giving tasks and responsibilities to the european commission. it is good that the european commission calls on member states to shoulder their responsibilities and to give them incentives, but the european commission should not take on the responsibilities of member states. the commission is already doing a lot to promote the integration of roma. it is the member states themselves that often do not do enough to integrate roma and to implement a national roma integration strategy.
in writing. - i have just abstained from voting on the gue/ngl group's resolution on tax evasion and avoidance. although i support many of the measures mentioned, i cannot agree with a european parliament resolution calling for, among other things, adjustments to income taxation. the eu is not about that.
